<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the Tripataki Chakra, focusing on the mathematical placement of planets—Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, the Sun, and Saturn—on the diagram to predict health and fortune for a specific year of a person's life.</meta>

<margin>Pa. Sva. 18 18</margin>

Regarding the **remainders** <note>original: "shesha"; the numerical remainder left over after a mathematical division, used here to determine planetary placement.</note>: the others—**Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, the Sun, and Saturn**—should be written as before, in positions corresponding to the remainder when divided by four. In this way, one should contemplate the auspicious and inauspicious effects occurring in those years. Here, the calculation for **Rahu** <note>The North Node of the Moon, depicted as a shadow planet.</note> and **Ketu** <note>The South Node of the Moon; both Rahu and Ketu are considered "malefics" in this system.</note> should be performed in reverse. Thus, one should examine the **Tripataki** <note>The "three-flagged" diagram used for health predictions.</note> through the **Ketu Chakra** <note>A specific variation of the chart centered on the influence of the South Node.</note>. 

Now, he explains a specific alternative method: Within the years of life, such as at the time of marriage and so on, one should place the **Ascendant** <note>original: "Lagna"; the zodiac sign rising at the moment of birth.</note> on the middle line. Having established the positions of the signs, the number of years passed since birth is added to the birth positions of the Sun and other planets. However, **Rahu** and **Ketu** are excluded from this addition. Here, the **Moon** is joined with the value of the elapsed years divided by nine. 

The remainders of four and four determine the auspicious or inauspicious nature of the year. **Rahu and Ketu** must be considered in the **Ketu Wheel** in a counter-clockwise direction. || 48 ||

If such an alternative is desired, it should be established elsewhere as possible. Here, the "obstruction" <term>vedha</term> <note>A technical term in Indian astrology referring to the "piercing" or interference of one planet's influence by another based on their positions on the diagram.</note> of the birth-sign must be considered by means of the planets. 

**The meaning of this is as follows:** 
The elapsed years are added to the positions of the planets as they were at the time of birth. Specifically, the number of elapsed years is divided by four, and the planets starting with the Sun are written in the zodiac signs corresponding to those remainders. However, **Rahu and Ketu** should be treated as exceptions; they are to be written in reverse order. Likewise, the calculation should be performed by adding the remainder of the elapsed years divided by nine [for the Moon]. This is the intent: the number of elapsed years should be written after being divided by nine. || 48 ||

# Now, the determination of the *Tripataki* piercing regarding the month is described.

Regarding the month: in that case, as before, after constructing the **Tripataki chart** <term>Tripataki-chakra</term>, one should place the **Monthly Ascendant** <term>Masa-lagna</term> on the central line. In the previously described manner, the nine planets starting with the **Sun** should be inscribed. Having understood the piercing <term>vedha</term> according to the methods stated for the **Moon**, **Mars**, **Sun**, and others, one should predict the results. 

There is a special rule regarding the placement of the **Moon**: it must be written according to the sequence of the Moon’s **ninth-part** <term>navamsha</term> <note>a 3°20' division of a zodiac sign</note> at birth. This means: one should calculate and write the Moon's position based on the specific ninth-part where the birth Moon was situated, in relation to the divisions of the days of the month. As follows:

> -> Month by month, the result is calculated by the portions of the Moon's sign. <-
> -> If the Moon is pierced by a malefic planet, it is destroyed [rendered powerless]. 58 <-

Month.

half.
</margin>
For example, if at birth the **Moon** is in the fifth ninth-part of the sign **Cancer**, then for the [first] half of the month, it should be written in the sign corresponding to the Monthly Ascendant. <margin>In the second half of the month</margin> In the second half of the month, it should be written in the sign corresponding to the following house. 

In this context, if the **Moon** is pierced by a malefic planet, it is then "destroyed"; this means it will not yield the desired results. It is also said: "In the year, month, day, and hour, one should state the result after knowing the piercing." 

Here is a specific detail: only if the **Lord of the Rod** <term>Dandesha</term> <note>A specific planetary ruler in this system used for timing longevity and obstacles</note> is a malefic at birth, and there is a piercing in the current year or month, should **death** be predicted. Otherwise, if the Lord of the Rod is not a malefic, one should only predict **suffering**. Now, regarding the statement on specific...

# On the Twenty-four Years

Regarding the twenty-four years: One should contemplate the maximum lifespan <term>paramayurdaya</term> beyond twenty-four years. One should investigate the calculation of longevity according to the method to be stated hereafter. Ordinary people do not understand this calculation of longevity because they lack certainty in...

<note>The following text is written to the right of the astrological diagram</note>

> -> One should contemplate the calculation of longevity through the twenty-four years. Ordinary people do not know this; it is certainly complex and difficult. 1. <-
> -> In ancient times, a decision was made by **Varahamihira** and other knowers of truth; following their purpose, I shall state a little of what I have understood. 2. <-

...why is it said to be impossible to know? Because it is difficult. 1. Regarding the phrase "by Varaha and others": this refers to the determination made in ancient times by the great teacher **Varahamihira** <note>a famous 6th-century Indian astronomer and astrologer</note> and other sages. Following their purpose and according to their methods, a small portion of what has been understood by me is now to be stated. 2.

Regarding the "unwavering" <note>original: *nishchalasya*</note>: Just as the true nature of one who is unwavering is known through their own character, so too is the decision of the sage known through the instruction of a teacher <term>gurupadesha</term>. Based on the planets situated in that house, one should predict the auspicious or inauspicious results for that year.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for calculating astrological periods. While the previous page introduced the Year Ascendant (Varsha Lagna) and the Sudarshana Chakra, this section shifts focus to identifying the "Lord of the Year" and the specific calendar math required to find the start of a person's astrological year, specifically within the month of Karttika.</meta>

Now, he explains the method for determining the **Lord of the Year** <term>Varshesha</term> <note>original: *varshesha*; the specific planet that acts as the primary ruler of a person's life for the duration of a single year</note>. Starting from the sign of the Lord of the Year, the year should be determined according to the calculation of the **lunar mansions** <term>Nakshatras</term>. Then, having identified the ruler according to the method to be explained, one should determine and declare the **results** <term>Phala</term> based on that ruler’s influence. 5.

Now, he describes the procedure for the **commencement of the year** <term>Varsharambha</term> starting from the month of **Karttika** <note>the eighth month of the Hindu calendar, typically falling in October–November; often associated with the start of the financial or liturgical year in various Indian traditions</note>.

> Starting from the sign of the Lord of the Year, and following the rule of the lunar mansion numbers,
> the year should be calculated, and the results should be determined accordingly. 5.
> Nine days before the end of Karttika, up to the day of the birth month,
> one should add these days and months to the elapsed year. 6.

Regarding the phrase "at the end of Karttika": the start of the year here follows the **civil calendar** <term>Savana</term> <note>original: *savana*; a time-keeping system where each month is fixed at 30 days and the year at 360 days, distinct from lunar or solar-sidereal months</note>. In this context, after the twenty-first day of Karttika <note>calculated as 30 days minus the 9 days mentioned in the verse</note>, the calculation is to be performed up to the limit of the birth month and birth day. The months and days that have passed therein should be applied to the results of the Lord of the Year. Furthermore, the commencement of the next year occurs on the seventh day of the bright half of Karttika <note>the *Shukla Paksha* or waxing phase of the moon</note>. 6.

# The System of the Ketu Pataki Banner

Now, he explains the placement of the **Ketu Pataki** <term>Ketu Pataki Nyasa</term> <note>original: *ketu patākī nyāsa*; an astrological technique where a "banner" (pataki) is used to map planetary lords to specific years based on the birth star</note> for the progression of the years. Starting from the **Sun** <term>Ravi</term> and counting through the **lunar mansions** <term>Nakshatras</term> starting with **Krittika** <note>the third lunar mansion in the standard list, but used as the starting point in this specific system; it corresponds to the Pleiades</note>, and repeating the sequence three times, the **Lord of the Year** <term>Varshesha</term> is to be determined. 

Specifically: for someone born under the lunar mansion of Krittika, the Lord of their first year is the **Sun** <term>Ravi</term>. For the second year, it is the **Moon** <term>Chandra</term>; then **Mars** <term>Bhauma</term>; then **Mercury** <term>Budha</term>; after that **Saturn** <term>Shani</term>; then **Jupiter** <term>Jiva</term>; then the North Node **Rahu**; and after that the South Node **Ketu**. Thus, the ninth year is ruled by **Venus** <term>Bhrugujita</term> <note>original: *bhṛgujita*; literally "born of Bhrigu," a common name for Venus</note>. 

-> The Sun, Moon, Mars, and Mercury; Saturn, Jupiter, Rahu, and Ketu. <-
-> Among the planets, the cycle occurs in order starting from Krittika. 7 <-

In this way, starting from the tenth year, the calculation should again be performed starting from the Sun. This calculation should be repeated again and again for subsequent years. It is also stated: 

Having written the sequence of the Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Saturn, Jupiter, Rahu, Ketu, and Venus <note>original: *arkendu-bhauma-jña-shani-ijya-rāhu-ketu-asphujit*</note> starting from the Agni-star <note>the star Krittika, whose presiding deity is Agni, the fire god</note> in the Banner chart three times, the Lord of the Year is determined by the years elapsed since birth. 

There is another instruction regarding the construction: the planetary circle <term>Graha Mandala</term> consists of the Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Saturn, Jupiter, Rahu, and Ketu. <insert>They are placed in the divisions starting from Krittika</insert> following the sequence, moving counter-clockwise in one part. 7.

# The Results of the Yearly Planetary Periods

Now, he explains the results of the yearly planetary periods <term>Varsha-Dasha-Phala</term> <note>original: *varṣadaśaphala*; the predicted outcomes for a specific year based on which planet is the "lord" of that year</note>. 

In the **Year of the Sun** <term>Ravi-Varsha</term>, there is travel in foreign lands, distress, and an increase in suffering; there is also fear, grief, and fever involving one's beloved <note>original: *kāntājvaraśokabhayāni*</note>. (Period: 8 units). 

In the **Year of the Moon** <term>Chandra-Varsha</term>, there is physical nourishment, fortitude, and contentment, as well as health, wealth, and the birth of children. (Period: 5 units).

In the **Year of Mars** <term>Bhauma-Varsha</term>...

By the influence of the **Sun** <term>Ravi</term>, one travels to foreign lands, experiences distress and bodily pain, and the planet causes illness, fever, and grief for one's spouse/beloved, leaving one filled with fear. (8)

By the influence of the **Moon** <term>Chandra</term>, there is nourishment, fortitude, and satisfaction; indeed, there is happiness and prosperity. One attains health and fulfills desires, accompanied by wealth and children. (5)

In the **Year of Mars** <term>Bhauma</term>, there is always disease of the vital organs <term>Marma-roga</term> <note>original: *marmaroga*; refers to ailments affecting sensitive or vulnerable points of the body</note> and the loss of work will certainly occur. There is a constant inclination toward wicked deeds and mental agony day after day. (9)

In the **Year of Mercury** <term>Budha</term>, there is wisdom/awakening, the gain of children, success in one's work, and great happiness. One is constantly honored by kings and experiences the gain of wealth day after day. (5)

---

In the **Year of Mars**, there is loss of work, wandering, fear, distress, and engagement in wicked deeds. (9)

In the **Year of Mercury**, there is knowledge, the gain of children, happiness, honor from kings, and the gain of wealth. (5)

In the period of the Moon <term>Vidhu</term> and Mercury <term>Budha</term>, there is auspiciousness; however, in the periods of the King <note>likely referring to the Sun</note> and Saturn <term>Manda</term>, death may occur. The duration of a major period <term>Dasha</term> should be multiplied by the duration of the sub-period <term>Antardasha</term> and then divided by the total sum of the period-units <term>Dasha-pinda</term>. The resulting quotient should be understood in days and so on; the remainder should be multiplied by sixty... (19)

...and divided again by the period-sum <term>Dasha-pinda</term>. In this same way, the <term>Pala</term> <note>a unit of time equal to 24 seconds</note> and further subdivisions can be found for the sub-sub-periods <term>Praty-antardasha</term> starting from the Sun <term>Arka</term> and the others in sequence. (20)

The sequence for placing the planets is as follows. Regarding the two planets:

The Sun, Mercury, Mars, Jupiter, Moon, Venus, as well as Rahu and Saturn, should be known for the Ketu horoscope (28). Between every two planets, there are four positions for Ketu. Related to the deity Ahirbudhnya <note>The celestial serpent and deity of the Uttarabhadrapada star</note>,

For example: for one whose birth star is Uttarabhadrapada, starting from the first year, the lord of the first three <gloss>years</gloss> is the Sun <term>Ravi</term>. Then, the lord of the fourth year is Ketu. Following that, the lords of the fifth and sixth years are Kala and Rahu. Then, starting from the seventh year...

...up to those years, Mercury <term>Budha</term> is the lord of three years. In this way, between the Sun and Mercury, there is one Ketu; and after Ketu, two "Time-Nodes" <note>original: "Kalarahu," a specialized astrological point often associated with the shadow planet Rahu but used here in a specific sub-divisional system</note> are to be known. 

The application is as follows: for a person born under the lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra</term> of Revati, the Sun <term>Ravi</term> is the lord of the first two years. After that, Ketu is the lord of the third year. Then, from the fourth year onwards, the "King of Time" <note>original: "Kalaraja"</note> is the master for two years. Following that, up to the sixth, seventh, and eighth year, Mercury <term>Budha</term> is the lord. 

Similarly, for one born in the star Ashwini, the Sun is the lord of the first year, and Ketu is the lord of the second. After that, starting from the third year, the King of Time is the master for two years. Following that, Mercury is the lord of three years. 

Then, after Mercury, Mars <term>Bhauma</term> is the lord of three years. After that, Ketu is the lord of one year. Then again, the King of Time is the lord of two years. Following that, Jupiter <term>Guru</term> is the lord of three years. After that, the Moon <term>Chandra</term> is the lord of three years. Then, Ketu is again the lord of one year. 

Here also, the King of Time is the lord of two years. Then, Venus <term>Bhrigu</term> is the lord of three years. After that, Rahu is the lord of three years. Then again, Ketu is the lord of one year. Here too, as before, the King of Time is the lord of two years. Then, Saturn <term>Shani</term> is the lord of three years. 

Thus, in the four intermediate positions between every two [major] planets, four Ketus are to be written. Everywhere, after a Ketu, two Time-Nodes <term>Kalarahu</term> are to be written. 

In this system, for one born in the star Uttarabhadrapada <note>original: "Abhaipada," a shorthand for Ahirbudhnya-pada/Uttarabhadrapada</note>, the Sun's own period comes first. Then, after that, three Saturns <note>periods ruled by Saturn</note> arise. Likewise, for one born in Revati, in the beginning there are two Sun [periods], and at the end, one Sun...

In the year of Ketu <term>Ketu-varsha</term>, there is fear of thieves and various kinds of bondage. In the year of Mercury <term>Budha</term>, there is wandering through foreign lands, disease, and fear of death. || 42 ||

Suffering regarding wife and children, grief, and the destruction of wealth occur. In the year of Mars <term>Bhauma-varsha</term>, there will be distress and fear from the agents of the god of death <note>original: "yamyaih"</note>, thieves, and fire. || 43 ||

In the year of Jupiter <term>Guru-varsha</term>, there will be acquisition, and in the year of the Moon <term>Chandra-varsha</term>, there are successes <gloss>the attainment of gold, silver, etc., and the arrival of wealth in the form of elephants and horses</gloss>. || 44 ||

In the year of Venus <term>Shukra-varsha</term>, there are cattle, vehicles, and riches. In the year of the King <note>original: "Raja," referring here to the Sun</note>, there is fear of water and the pain of sores on the feet. || 45 ||

—[these years bring] success in agriculture and gains in gold, silver, and happiness. || 44 || As before regarding Ketu: In the year of Venus, many cattle, the gain of horses and vehicles, etc., and all types of happiness. In the year of the King (the Sun), fear of water, sores on the feet, and the destruction of one's position or home, etc. || 45 ||

> A wise man should repeatedly arrange the group of stars beginning with Pushya in sequence.

Under the Sun is Pushya; under Mars is Ashlesha; then under Ketu is Magha; under the Moon is Purva-phalguni; under Mercury is Uttara-phalguni; under Jupiter is Hasta; under Venus <note>original: "Bhrigu", a lineage name for Venus</note> is Chitra; under Saturn is Svati; and under Rahu is Vishakha. Having written these nine stars, one should write the next nine stars starting with Anuradha under the Sun and others using the same method. "The Sun, Mars <note>original: "Arki-su", possibly a corruption for "Arka-suta" or "Aara", both referring to Mars</note>, Ketu, Moon, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, Saturn, and Rahu are the planets. The birth star placed in its corresponding cell indicates the planet born of the Teacher's Chart." [49]

# Now he describes the results for the Lord of the Year.

The results for the pairs beginning with the Sun are clarified in other texts. The influence of Mercury and the Moon is auspicious. There is emptiness <note>original: "sunyata", perhaps implying a lack of results or a neutral state</note> for the King and Saturn, while happiness comes from the mentors of the gods and the demons <note>Jupiter and Venus, respectively</note>. Happiness is also found in the pairs of Mars and Rahu. Regarding the Teacher’s Horoscope Chart <term>Guru-Kundali</term>, if its results are analyzed through the various sequences, a great doubt or confusion arises in three places. Therefore, to understand the results of the Lord of the Year <term>Varsha-isha</term>, he now explains the "Unified Wheel" of the three horoscopes mentioned previously. To illustrate this, here is an example:

In the year **Samvat 1806** (Shaka 1671) <note>approximately 1749 CE</note>, during the Sun's northern course <term>Uttarayan</term>, in the month of Vaishakha, on the twelfth day of the bright fortnight <term>Shukla Dvadashi</term>, on a Tuesday...

->A year ruled by the Sun brings sorrow, and the same for a year of Mars.<-
->In the period of Ketu, there is distress; whereas happiness comes in the years of the Moon and Mercury. [5]<-

...at 26 ghatis and 33 palas <note>units of time in the Hindu calendar; 60 ghatis make a day</note>, the moon was in the lunar mansion of **Uttara Phalguni** (9;19). The alignment <term>Yoga</term> was Harshana (00;19), and the temporal period <term>Karana</term> was Kaulava. According to the almanac <term>Panchanga</term>, the length of the day was 32 ghatis and 30 palas. The Sun's position was at 0 signs, 9 degrees, 35 minutes, and 33 seconds. At the desired time of 37 ghatis and 14 palas, someone was born under the rising sign of Scorpio <term>Vrischika Lagna</term>. For this person, the birth star <term>Nakshatra</term> is **Hasta**. In the Ketu-Flag chart <term>Ketu-Pataki</term>, the Moon is positioned above that star. Therefore, for the first year, the Lord of the Year is the Moon. Furthermore, in the Ketu chart, the Moon is positioned over the birth star; because of this, for the first year and the second year, the Lord is the Moon. || 11

Pandit Sva.

29
</margin>

Then, in the Teacher’s Chart <term>Guru-Kundali</term>, Jupiter acts as the "light of birth" <note>original: "janma-dipti," likely referring to a starting point or a primary influence in the horoscope</note>. From that point, Jupiter becomes the Lord of the Year for the first year and the third year. After writing down the Lords of the Year in this sequence, for whatever year the analysis is being conducted, one should identify the three current Year-Lords starting from the birth year. Among those three, the planet that possesses the greatest strength—endowed with its own brilliance and favorable positioning—is the one that will yield results during the first four months.

->The rule of Jupiter and Venus brings happiness; there is great distress under Saturn.<-
->The same [distress] is to be understood for Rahu, as stated by Varahamihira <note>A famous 6th-century Indian astronomer and astrologer</note> and others. [5]<-

In those months, that planet should be known as the giver of results. Following that, the planet with slightly less strength gives favorable results during the middle four months. Finally, the planet with the least strength among them is the one that gives results in the final four months. Alternatively, if a single planet appears in two or three of these positions, it should be recognized as the giver of results right from the beginning, regardless of its specific strength rating. As it is said: "Between the Lord of the Year’s Entry and the Lord of the Banner <term>Pataki-adhisha</term> <note>a specific calculation used in Tajik-influenced Indian astrology</note>, whichever among them is the strongest, or if one occupies two or three positions, the year must be divided according to that planet's influence." [5]

--- Page 80 ---
<meta>Continuing from the previous page, which established the calculation of the "Lord of the Year" (Varsha-isha) and its relative strengths, this page explains how to divide the astrological year into three distinct periods by consulting three different charts: the Ketu-Banner, the Ketu-Chart, and the Jupiter-Chart.</meta>

Now, concerning the **combination of three malefics** <note>original: "pāpa-traya-yoga"</note>, he explains the specific details regarding the Banner <term>Pataki</term> <note>A specialized diagnostic chart used in Tajika or annual horoscopy, often visualized as a flag or banner</note> system. In this regard, first, the **Ketu-Banner Chart** <note>original: "Ketu-patākī-cakra"</note> should be analyzed. Next, the **Ketu-Chart** <term>Ketu-kundali</term> should be understood. After that, the **Jupiter-Chart** <term>Guru-kundali</term> should be understood. Thus, by observing these three charts and the combination of auspicious and inauspicious influences, one should describe the good and bad results in their proper order.

For example: if the **Lord of the Year** <term>Varsh-esha</term> is auspicious in the Ketu-Banner Chart, then a good result should be predicted for the first third of the year—that is, up to the end of the **first four months**. Similarly, in the second chart, the Ketu-Chart, if the Lord of the Year is auspicious, then during the middle third of the year—that is, from the fourth month...

---

->The Banner chart, the chart of Ketu, and the chart of Jupiter;<-
->If there is a combination of malefics in all of these, a great uncertainty arises.<-
<note>Verse number 52</note>
५२

---

...up until the **eighth month**, the results should be known as auspicious. Furthermore, if the Lord of the Year is auspicious in the Jupiter-Chart, then for the **final third**—that is, from after the eighth month until the end of the year—excellent and auspicious results should be predicted.

In this manner, if there is a mixture of auspicious and inauspicious combinations across these three charts, a mixed result should be described following the order mentioned. Moreover, if there is a "combination of malefics everywhere"—meaning that if the Year-Lords in all three charts are malefic planets—then a "great uncertainty" or a **major misfortune** <term>Arishta</term> <note>A technical term for planetary combinations indicating ill-health, tragedy, or significant life obstacles</note> should be understood. ॥ ५२ ॥

<summary>This page details a method of annual prediction where the year is divided into three four-month segments, each governed by a different chart (Ketu-Pataki, Ketu-Kundali, and Guru-Kundali). It warns that if malefic planets dominate all three charts, the subject faces significant peril or "Arishta."</summary>

--- Page 82 ---
<meta>This folio continues the technical discussion of astrological divination systems. While the previous page focused on the Pataki (Banner) system and planetary cycles of Ketu and Jupiter, this page introduces a new technique: the Five-Vowel Wheel (Pancha-swara-chakra) used for predicting misfortune or death (Arishta-nirnaya).</meta>

<page-num>54</page-num>

Now, for the determination of ill-omens <term>ariṣṭa-nirṇaya</term> <note>Specifically, methods used in Indian astrology to predict the timing of misfortune or the end of life.</note>, the author explains the method of the **Five-Vowel Wheel** <term>pañca-svara-cakra</term>. First, to understand the relationship between letters and vowels, the **Vowel Wheel** is presented. 

The author of this specific text has not described the "gateway" <note>An introductory explanation or entry-point to the system.</note> here; however, it should be understood from the treatise titled *The Victory-Step of the King* <note>original: "Narapatijayacaryā," a foundational 12th-century Sanskrit text on phonetics and prognostications.</note>. It is as follows: "From 'ka' to 'ha'..." in thirty-five [cells].

->**The Verse**<-

Write the letters starting with **ka** and ending with **ha** beneath the vowels, excluding **ṅa**, **ña**, and **ṇa** <note>The nasal consonants of the first three phonetic groups in the Sanskrit alphabet.</note>. These should be arranged in horizontal rows within thirty-five cells. **53**

Whichever vowel the first letter of a person’s name stands beneath, that vowel is designated here as the **Letter-Vowel** <term>varṇa-svara</term> for that specific letter. **54**

->**The Commentary**<-

The construction [of Verse 53] is as follows: In a wheel comprised of thirty-five cells, one should write the consonants from **ka** to **ha** in horizontal rows, leaving out the nasals **ṅa**, **ña**, and **ṇa**. **53**

Regarding the phrase "the person's name": whatever is the very first letter of a man's name, whichever vowel (beginning with the short 'a') that letter is situated beneath, that vowel becomes the **Letter-Vowel** <term>varṇa-svara</term> within this science—this is the intended meaning.

<summary>This page introduces the Five-Vowel Wheel (Panchaswara-chakra), a divinatory system that assigns specific vowels to the letters of a person's name to determine astrological outcomes and portents of death.</summary>
<keywords>Panchaswara-chakra, Arishta-nirnaya, Varnaswara, Narapatijayacharya, name divination, Sanskrit phonetics, astrological omens</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the "Five-Vowel Wheel" (Pancha-swara-chakra). While the previous page established how to identify a person's "Letter-Vowel" based on the first letter of their name, this page defines the specific names and symbolic states (such as life stages) assigned to each of the five vowels in the sequence.</meta>

<margin>
Five Vowels
31
31
</margin>

Now, the author declares the **designations of the five vowels** <term>svarāṇām saṃjñā</term>. Whichever "Letter-Vowel" belongs to a specific man, that vowel is given the designation **Rising** <term>udita</term> <note>original: "udita," literally "risen," like the sun appearing on the horizon.</note>. The second vowel from that one is designated **Wandering** <term>bhramita</term>; the third is designated **Confused** <term>bhrānta</term>; the fourth is **Twilight** <term>sandhyā</term>; and the fifth is **Set** <term>asta</term> <note>original: "asta," referring to the setting of a celestial body.</note>.

Next, he provides alternative sets of names for these same five states, starting with the "Rising" vowel: **Birth**, **Action**, and **Conception**.

->**Rising, Wandering, Confused, Twilight, and then Set.**<-
->**Birth, Action, Conception, Body, and then Defect.** 55<-
->**From the first letter of the name, the five states—**<-
->**Child and the rest—are known: Child, Adolescent,**<-
->**Young Man, Aged, or even Dead. 56**<-

The designations **Body** <term>piṇḍa</term> and **Defect** <term>chidra</term> <note>original: "chidra" literally means a hole or a vulnerability; in this context, it refers to a point of weakness or fatality.</note> should be understood in that specific order. **55**

Again, he provides yet another set of designations: based on the first letter of a person’s name, one should recognize the five life-stages beginning with **Child**. The first vowel is the **Child** <term>bāla</term>; the second is the **Adolescent** <term>kumāra</term>; the third is the **Young Man** <term>yuvā</term>; the fourth is the **Aged** <term>vṛddha</term>; and the fifth is the **Dead** <term>mṛta</term>. **56**

<insert>Each state yields results according to its own inherent nature; of this there is no doubt.</insert> <note>This later addition emphasizes that the vowel's "state"—whether it is "Child" or "Dead"—directly dictates the astrological favorability or misfortune for the person.</note>

### The Results of the "Rising" and Other Vowel-States

Now, the author declares the results of the **Rising** and other vowel-states. Regarding the **Rising** <term>udita</term> state: when a person's vowel is in the Rising state, there is always **victory** in all undertakings. In the **Wandering** <term>bhramita</term> state, there are **gains** <note>original: "lābhāḥ," referring to profit or acquisition.</note>. In the **Confused** <term>bhrānta</term> state, there is **success** <term>siddhi</term> <note>original: "siddhi" implies the successful completion or perfection of a task.</note>. In the **Reached Twilight** <term>sandhyāpta</term> state, also called the "Twilight-Designated," death occurs with certainty. In the *Jaya* <note>original: "jaiye," likely referring to the *Jayayoddha-Swarodaya*, a foundational text of this tradition.</note> and in other treatises, special results are given regarding the year, month, day, lunar day <term>tithi</term>, lunar mansion <term>nakṣatra</term>, and day-of-the-week when that Twilight state is reached.

->**Rising always brings victory, and Wandering brings gain;**<-
->**In the Confused state, one attains success; in Twilight, death—**<-
->**is certain. 57**<-

Because of this <note>referring to the influence of the Twilight/Death state</note>, great inauspiciousness arises: loss, sorrow, great suffering, and the destruction of wealth occur. Death from an assault, a fall, or similar accidents happens on that day for certain. Furthermore, any disease that arises in a man on that specific day will become incurable <term>asādhya</term>, bringing the pain of death and other such miseries. 57

<summary>This page details the auspicious and inauspicious outcomes associated with the five vowel-states, warning that the "Twilight" state leads to certain death, loss of wealth, and incurable illness.</summary>
<keywords>Swarodaya, Udita (Rising), Bhramita (Wandering), Bhrānta (Confused), Sandhyāpta (Twilight), victory, siddhi (success), prognosis, incurable disease</keywords>

### Calculation of the Time Divisions

Now, the author explains the first state of existence. Where—meaning in whichever vowel— the first letter of a person’s name <term>nāmādimavarṇa</term> <note>original: "nāmādimavarṇo," the initial phoneme of a given name.</note> is found, that is where the **Rising** vowel <term>udita-swara</term> occurs. From that specific vowel, the beginning of the year <note>original: "varṣārambho," though transcribed here as "darṣārambho," clearly refers to the start of the year.</note> is to be known. Furthermore, from that year, the month is determined. 58

He explains how the month is determined: "Two months" and "twelve etc." <note>This refers to the calculation used to divide the year into five equal parts.</note>

> **Where the letter of the name is found, there is the Rising vowel;**
> **From that, one should know the year; from the year, the month occurs**
> **again. 58 Two months are to be assigned, along with twelve**
> **additional days. In this sequence, the fivefold division of the**
> **year is to be understood. 59**

The period of two months and twelve additional days <note>2 months (60 days) + 12 days = 72 days. This is one-fifth of a 360-day year.</note> should be understood to belong to the Rising vowel. The same duration applies to the **Wandering** <term>bhramita</term> and the other states as well. In this sequence, the fivefold division of the year is understood; this means it should be known that the year consists of five parts, each the length of five months <note>This commentary likely interprets the "five parts" as the total structure of the year governed by the five vowels.</note>. 59

<vocab>prathama-sattā (first state), swara (vowel/sound), nāmākṣara (letter of the name), varṣa (year), māsa (month), vidhātavya (to be assigned), krameṇa (in sequence)</vocab>

<meta>This page continues the specific mathematical breakdown of the 360-day year into five equal segments of 72 days each. These segments are assigned to the five vowels (A, I, U, E, O) to determine the astrological influence on an individual based on their name-vowel.</meta>

The author clarifies this point with a verse and a half. Beginning with the month of **Kartika** <note>The eighth month of the Hindu calendar, typically October–November.</note>, in the latter part—specifically during the bright fortnight <note>The waxing phase of the moon.</note>—for the first nine days; then the months of **Margashirsha** and **Pausha** in their entirety; and three days of **Magha**. In this sequence, it should be known how the year is completed.

Here is the arrangement: 
Nine days of **Kartika**, thirty days of **Margashirsha**, thirty days of **Pausha**, and three days of **Magha**. Thus, seventy-two days <note>original: "dvisapratidināni"</note> are situated under the vowel **A** <term>Akāra</term>. 

Furthermore: twenty-seven days of **Magha**, thirty days of **Phalguna**, and fifteen days of **Chaitra**. Thus, seventy-two days are under the vowel **I** <term>Ikāra</term>. Similarly, the remaining portion of **Chaitra**, which is...

->In the latter part of Kartika, nine days are taken;<-
->likewise Margashirsha and Pausha are given,<-
->and three days of Magha. 60<-

...fifteen days, then thirty days of **Vaishakha**, and twenty-seven days of **Jyeshtha**. Thus, seventy-two days are under the vowel **U** <term>Ukāra</term>. 

Furthermore: the final three days of **Jyeshtha**, thirty days of **Ashadha**, thirty days of **Shravana**, and nine days of **Bhadrapada**. Thus, seventy-two days are under the vowel **E** <term>Ekāra</term>. 

Then, twenty-one days of **Bhadrapada**, thirty days of **Ashvina**, and twenty-one days of **Kartika**. Thus, seventy-two days are to be placed under the vowel **O** <term>Okāra</term>. 

In this way, the year consisting of five divisions is to be understood. Here, the mention of "starting with the vowel A" is illustrative <term>upalakṣaṇa</term>; the sequence of **Rising** <term>Udita</term> and the other states should be understood according to one's own specific name-vowel. And it is further said:

The lunar days starting from the beginning of the bright fortnight <note>original: "śuklādeḥ"</note> are then calculated in accordance with these values. 63, 64.

### Method for Half-Grouping Calculations

Now the author describes the method for calculating the "half-grouping" <note>a division used to determine planetary influence</note> using the **Anushtubh** meter. Regarding **Age** <term>vaya</term>: count the number of years from the time of birth until the current year. To this sum, add the number corresponding to the person's birth zodiac sign <term>janma rashi</term>, counting from **Taurus** <term>Vrisha</term> as the first sign. For instance, Taurus is assigned the number 1, and **Aries** <term>Mesha</term> is assigned the number 12. In this same way, the numerical values for all twelve signs should be determined.

Combine the values for age, zodiac sign, and vowels, and place the sum in three separate positions. Multiply these by two, four, and three, and then divide by seven, eight, and six <note>original: "rasa," a poetic term for the number six</note>. 65 In the "Conception" position, nine should be added; in the "Vowel" position, five should be added. The corresponding lunar day <term>tithi</term> is then found in the grid beneath that vowel, whether in the waxing or waning fortnight.

--- Page 92 ---
<meta>This page follows the technical calendar calculations of the previous page. While the previous section mapped the 72-day blocks to specific months, this section transitions into calculating numerical values (Tithi-ankas) associated with specific lunar days to populate a "Birth Chart" (Janma-Koshtha). This involves complex arithmetic using remainders (modulo operations) based on the lunar calendar.</meta>

By unifying the individual units, the <term>Tithi-ankas</term> <note>literally "lunar day numbers"; numerical values assigned to specific days of the moon's cycle for use in astrological calculations</note> are produced. As is stated, for the <term>Shukla Pratipada</term> <note>the 1st day of the waxing moon</note>, the value is 1. Likewise, for the 6th day, it is 6; for the 11th, it is 11. For the <term>Krishna Pratipada</term> <note>the 1st day of the waning moon</note>, the value is 16; for the 6th, it is 21; and for the 11th, it is 26. In this manner, the sum of all these units becomes eighty-one (81). <note>These days (1st, 6th, 11th of both fortnights) belong to the "Nanda" or "Joyous" category of lunar days. 1+6+11+16+21+26 = 81.</note>

Regarding this, the Teacher has said in a previous verse, "Chesashtau" <note>original: "चेशष्टाविति" - likely a mnemonic reference to a specific calculation or total</note>. Similarly, for the <term>Bhadra</term> <note>"Lucky" days: 2nd, 7th, 12th</note> days, the total number becomes eighty-seven (87). For the <term>Jaya</term> <note>"Victorious" days: 3rd, 8th, 13th</note> days, the total is ninety-three (93). For the <term>Rikta</term> <note>"Empty" days: 4th, 9th, 14th</note> days, the number is ninety-nine (99). For the <term>Purna</term> <note>"Full" days: 5th, 10th, 15th</note> days, the total is one hundred and five (105). Thus, the numerical values for all the lunar days are established.

Now, having unified these stated values with the <term>Vayorashi</term> <note>a technical sum likely derived from the subject's age or a specific numerical group used in this system</note>, one should place them in three positions. That is to say, <margin>with four</margin> set them down in three places. <gloss>with four</gloss> Furthermore, these are multiplied by two, three, and four. <gloss>This is the intent:</gloss> In the first position, multiply the sum by two and divide by seven; place the remainder in the [first] cell of the <term>Janma-Koshtha</term> <note>the "Birth Chart" or "Nativity Grid" used in this specific predictive system</note>.

Again, take that same total <margin>four</margin> and in the second position, multiply it by three and divide by eight; place the remainder below that [in the second cell] of the Birth Chart. Then, <margin>three</margin> take that same total in the third position, multiply it by four and divide by six; place the remainder in the lowest cell of the Birth Chart. Having thus prepared the Birth Chart, one should then calculate the <term>Karma-Koshtha</term> <note>the "Action Chart," likely used to predict timing for specific deeds or events</note>. For example, regarding conception...

# Page 35

Based on the rules stated, the logical sequence of meaning is considered stronger than the literal sequence of the text. Therefore, to determine the **Table of Action** <term>Karma-koshtha</term><note>A specific grid or cell in the astrological chart related to one's profession or deeds</note>, one should take the previously mentioned sum <term>Pindaka</term> and add **five (5)** to it. Multiply these by two, three, and four, respectively. Take the value multiplied by two and divide it by seven; take the one multiplied by three and divide it by eight; and take the one multiplied by four and divide it by six. The three remainders obtained from these operations should be placed in the **upper, middle, and lower** sections of the Table of Action.

Now, the procedure for the **Table of Conception** <term>Adhana-koshtha</term><note>The cell representing the moment of conception</note> is described. To the previously mentioned sum, add **nine (9)**. <margin>Place in three positions</margin> As before, place this in three locations and multiply them by the same multipliers mentioned above. Then, divide them by the same divisors. The three remainders resulting from this should be placed in the Table of Conception, just as before.

Next, the procedure for the **Summary Table** <term>Pinda-koshtha</term> is described. These constants—**zero (0)**, **five (5)**, and **nine (9)** <note>original: "सून्य पंचनवरूपान्" - 0 for Birth, 5 for Action, 9 for Conception, which total 14</note>—should be added (totalling fourteen) to the previously mentioned sum. Multiply this by two, three, and four, and divide by the same divisors as before. The three remainders produced should be placed in the specified table in the three designated positions in the proper order.

<meta>This page continues the specific mathematical calculation for the "Gap Cell" (Chidra-koshtha). It provides an algorithm involving the sum of constant numbers and demonstrates the process with a concrete example involving the current year (76), the zodiac sign Virgo, and the Hasta lunar mansion.</meta>

"Situated"—the meaning of this is a series or a row, specifically referring to the constants <term>Dhruva-anka</term><note>Fixed numerical values used in astrological tables that do not change during a specific set of calculations</note>. It means a row of these constants. Therefore, in the **Gap Cell** <term>Chidra-koshtha</term> for birth, action, and conception, the row of constants derived from the **Summary Cell** <term>Pinda-koshtha</term> is 0, 5, 9, and 14. Their total sum, which is **twenty-eight (28)**, should be added to the previously mentioned base sum <term>Pinda</term>. 

Then, multiply these individually and divide by **seven, eight, and six** <note>original: "सप्ताष्टरस" (saptāṣṭarasa). In the Katapayadi system and Vedic numeracy, 'Rasa' (flavors) represents the number 6</note>. Taking the three remainders resulting from the division, one should place them in the Gap Cell in the order of **top, middle, and bottom**. 

As the authority **Vishveshvara** says:
"The wise person, having added fourteen to the base sum... twice twenty-eight... multiplied by two, four, and three, and divided by seven, eight, and six." <insert>75</insert> <insert>#</insert>

### An Example of This
Consider the current year as the **seventy-sixth (76)**. The zodiac sign is **Virgo** <term>Kanya</term>. Counting from Taurus <term>Vrisha</term>, the sign-sum is 5. Because this falls under the second quarter <term>charana</term> of the **Hasta** lunar mansion <note>One of the 27 divisions of the sky in Hindu astrology</note>, the syllables *Ma* and *U* are positioned below. Below those, the vowel-value is *E*, which represents 3. 

The sum of all these factors results in a base sum <term>Pinda</term> of **74**. This value is set down in three different places and multiplied by two, three, and four:
* In the first place, multiplied by 2, it becomes **148**.
* In the second place, multiplied by 3, it becomes **222**.
* In the third place, multiplied by 4, it becomes **296**.

Now for the division:
1. In the first place, when divided by **7**, the remainder is **5**.
2. In the second place, when **222** is divided by **8**, the remainder is **2**.
3. In the third place, when **296** is divided by **6**, the remainder is **zero (0)**.

Thus, the three remainders obtained are **5 | 2 | 0**.

One should prepare and write out the **Moving Chart** <term>Chalachakra</term> in this manner. After setting down the values in one's own appropriate Moving Chart cells—5, 2, and 00—and combining them with the values from the **Natural Chart** <term>Naisargika Chakra</term> cells, one should add the fixed constants <term>dhruvakas</term>. In the Moving Chart, the values for the **Birth** <term>Janma</term> cell are 5, 2, and 00. The values for the **Natural Chart** <term>Naisargika Chakra</term> cell are 2, 5, and 0. Their sum is 7, 7, and 0. Having added the fixed constant found there, and following the rule "one should multiply by those same multipliers," they are multiplied by two, three, and four, resulting in 4, 2, and 0. These are divided by seven, eight, and six; the remainders are 00, 5, and 00. These are then placed in the **Birth** cell of the **Combined Chart** in the top, middle, and bottom positions as 0, 5, and 00.

Next, the values for the **Action** <term>Karma</term> cell in the Moving Chart are 5, 00, and 00. The values for the Action cell in the Natural Chart are 2 and 12. Their sum is 6 and 12. With the addition of the fixed constants, the totals become 11, 6, and 7. These are multiplied by two, three, and four, resulting in 22, 18, and 28. Divided by seven, eight, and six, the [remainders] are 2, 2, and 4. These are placed in the **Action** cell as 2, 2, and 4.

Again, having combined the values for the **Conception** <term>Adhana</term> cell—5, 00, 00 and 2, 5, 00—the sum is 7, 5, and 00. Adding the fixed constant 4 and 9 found there, the total is 13 and 14. These are multiplied by two, three, and four, resulting in 32, 42, and 36.

<meta>This page continues the astrological instructions from the previous page, moving from the general results of the planetary lords to specific calculations for determining the timing of death. The previous page discussed the results of the "Natural" and "Moving" cycles; this page introduces the "Five-Vowel Cycle" (Pancha-Svara-Chakra) to narrow down the specific month and day.</meta>

Now, having established the **Five-Vowel Cycle** <term>Pancha-Svara-Chakra</term> in this manner, the author describes the method for determining the specific lunar day <term>tithi</term> and other time-measures of death, once the year of death has been ascertained through the period-combinations <term>dasha-yogas</term> which are to be explained. 

By calculating—meaning, having first determined the specific year of death and knowing the certainty of that passing—the "division of the year" <term>varsha-bhaga</term> must be performed. This means one must conceptualize in which specific part of the year death will occur, within a seventy-two day interval <note>original: "disaptati," referring to a 72-day period which is 1/5th of a 360-day year</note> starting from the **Risen** <term>Udita</term> state and so on. 

That calculation is as follows: take the previously mentioned numbers for the age, the zodiac sign, and the lord <note>original: "vayo-rashi-shvara-ankan"</note>, combine them into a single sum, and divide that total by five. Based on the remainder of one or more, death should be predicted in the seventy-two day month-period belonging to the vowel-lord of the **Risen** state and its successors. 

->Having calculated and determined the year,<-
->the division of the year is envisioned;<-
->when divided by five, the remainder indicates<-
->the start of the cause of death. 67.<-

The meaning is as follows: if the remainder is **one**, death occurs in the first part of the year mentioned before (the Risen state). If the remainder is **two**, it is in the **Wandering** <term>Bhramita</term> part of the year. If the remainder is **three**, it is in the **Confused** <term>Bhranta</term> part of the year. If the remainder is **four**, it is in the **Twilight** <term>Sandhya</term> part of the year. If the remainder is **five** (or zero), it is in the **Set** <term>Asta</term> part of the year. 

In whichever of these seventy-two day periods it is determined to occur, one must then look at the three cells <term>kostha</term> belonging to that vowel-lord. Whichever cell is the weakest <note>original: "pyunyam," likely referring to the lowest numerical value or 'void' state</note>, death will occur within that junction <term>sandhi</term> of twenty-four days...

<summary>This page explains how to use the Five-Vowel Cycle to narrow down the timing of death to a 72-day window and subsequently a 24-day window. ...death is to be predicted. Now, in all these cases, death [occurs] as follows:

<margin>E</margin>
If there are two [strong factors], then in the middle of the three hundred [days], whichever is strong, death occurs within twenty-four days <note>original: "chaturvinshati dinabhyantare," referring to a further subdivision of time</note> when that factor is in an empty state or positioned in an empty house. If there is an absence of such "emptiness" among them, death occurs within twenty-four days related to the Sun, Mars, Saturn, and the Darkness <term>Tamas; a name for the shadow-planet Rahu</term>. If there is an absence of these malefic planets <term>papas</term> in those positions, then death occurs within twenty-four days related to the benefic planets <term>saumya-graha</term> that were located in the angles <term>kendra; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</term>, trines <term>kona; the 5th and 9th houses</term>, or the fourth house <term>chaturasra</term> at the time of birth.

Regarding this, the author describes the means of knowing the **Nanda** and other lunar days <term>tithis</term> by the phrase "by five." By dividing the previously mentioned total sum <term>pinda</term> by five—

->The lunar day should be multiplied by six,<-

When the remainder is one or more, the **Nanda** group of days is to be known as the cause of death. Specifically:
*   If the remainder is **one**, it is a **Nanda** day.
*   If the remainder is **two**, it is a **Bhadra** day.
*   If the remainder is **three**, it is a **Jaya** day.
*   If the remainder is **four**, it is a **Rikta** day.
*   If the remainder is **five**, it is a **Purna** <note>original: "complete"</note> day. 67.

By the phrase "by six"—when the total sum <term>pinda</term> is divided by six, if the remainder is **one**, it occurs on the first Nanda, which is the first day of the bright fortnight <note>original: "shukla pratipada"</note>. If the remainder is **two**, it occurs on the sixth day <note>original: "shashthyam"</note>. If the remainder is **three**...

<meta>This page continues the specific mathematical rules for predicting the timing of death. Having previously established the 24-day window, the text now focuses on the "Pinda" (total sum) method to calculate the exact lunar day (tithi), the weekday (vara), the rising sign (lagna), and the specific time-unit (danda) of passing.</meta>

# Four

...if there is a remainder, death [occurs] on the eleventh day of the waning moon. If the remainder is **four**, it is a **Nanda** day <term>Nanda-tithi; the auspicious 'delighting' lunar days—the 1st, 6th, or 11th of a fortnight</term>. If the remainder is **five**, it occurs on the first day of the dark fortnight <term>Krishna Pratipada</term>. If the remainder is **six**, it occurs on the sixth day of the dark fortnight. If the remainder relates to the eleventh day of the dark fortnight, death is to be spoken of <note>original: "mrityur vachyah"</note>. Thus, in the first portion, if the remainder is two, the death occurs in the first major period <term>dasha</term>; otherwise, it occurs in the second or subsequent periods.

In all cases, some authorities say that according to the previously mentioned method of halving, death occurs when the breath falls on a specific vowel, or specifically on the **Nanda** lunar day.

### Determining the Weekday

Now the author explains the method for finding the weekday: By dividing the total sum <term>pinda</term> by seven, the remainders starting from one indicate the days of the week, beginning with Sunday. Regarding the phrase "by seven and five," the intent is as follows: 
*   If the remainder is **one**, the day belongs to the Sun or Mars.
*   If **two**, it belongs to the Moon or Mercury.
*   If **three**, it belongs to Jupiter.
*   If **four**, it belongs to Venus.
*   If **five**, it belongs to Saturn.

### Determining the Ascendant

Now, for the knowledge of the rising sign <term>Lagna; the zodiac sign on the eastern horizon at the moment of death</term>: When the total sum is divided by sixty and the remainder is then divided by twelve, the resulting number corresponds to the sign.
*   If the remainder is **one**, it is Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note>.
*   If the remainder is **twelve**, it is Pisces <note>original: "Meena"</note>.

### Determining the Specific Hour

Now the author explains the knowledge of the time-unit <term>Danda; a unit of time equal to 24 minutes, also called a ghati</term>: Divide the sum by the duration of the determined rising sign; the remainder indicates the specific "rod" or hour-unit of that sign when death is to be predicted. 

In cases where the weekday or other factors seem to conflict, the tradition <note>original: "sampradaya"</note> holds that one should designate the lunar day <term>tithi</term> as the primary cause of death. 68.

<page-num>40</page-num>

Now the author explains the refinement for calculating the lunar day based on the influence of the Moon:

If the Moon <term>Chandra</term> was full at the time of birth, **one** should be added to the total sum <term>pinda-anka; the numerical value used for calculation</term>. When the Moon is waning <note>original: "kshina", meaning diminished or weak</note>, then **one** should be subtracted or discarded from the total sum. Again, if the Moon is in the middle phase between waxing and waning, nothing should be added or subtracted.

The arrangement for the Full, Waning, and Intermediate Moon is as follows:
*   From the **tenth day of the waxing moon** <term>Shukla Dashami</term> until the **fifth day of the waning moon** <term>Krishna Panchami</term>, the Moon is considered **Full** <term>Purnendu</term>.
*   From the **sixth day of the waning moon** until the **end of the dark fortnight** <term>Amanta; the New Moon</term>, the Moon is **Waning**.
*   From the **first day of the waxing moon** <term>Shukla Pratipada</term> until the **tenth day**, the Moon is **Intermediate** <term>Madhyama</term>.

This concludes the sixty-ninth rule. Now, the sixteen combinations <term>Yogas</term>.

> **One** should be added or subtracted in the yearly collection of lunar days;
> Or, neither should be added nor subtracted if it falls in the middle of the waxing and waning phases. 69
> If the Sun, Saturn, or Mars <note>original: "Ravi-Manda-Kuja"</note> are in the year [chart], or if Rahu <term>the North Node of the Moon</term> is in three specific positions;
> Or if the "Seven-Void" <term>Sapta-shunya; a specific mathematical result of zero or seven</term> occurs, death happens and not otherwise. 70

### We Shall Now Explain

Among the three charts <term>chakras</term>, the timing of death is to be deliberated upon using the **Yogic-chakra**. As mentioned previously, if the Sun, Saturn, and Mars are the lords of the year in the three "Banner" charts <term>Pataki-traya</term>, and if Rahu is in those three positions, and if furthermore in the Yogic-chart there is a "Seven-Void" result, then death is certain.

Furthermore:
The phrase concerning the "Seven-Void" means that while others make a <margin>cry of "Ha-ha!"</margin> <gloss>at the moment of death</gloss> lamenting the separation, the soul <note>original: "hansa", literally 'swan', a metaphor for the soul</note> departs. Having previously <gloss>disregarded</gloss> the laughter and play of the world, the soul's journey <gloss>the breath or life-force</gloss> surely leads to heaven. 70

## The Combination for the End

The author explains the **Combination for the End** <note>original: "Antyayoga"; a specific planetary alignment indicating the end of life</note>. Regarding **Jupiter** <note>original: "Gurau"</note>: When, among the **Five Vowels** <term>Pancha-svara; a system where destiny is mapped onto the vowels A, I, U, E, O</term>, Jupiter is at the beginning, joined by **two zeros** <term>Shunya-dvaya; mathematical "voids" in the calculation that suggest a loss of vitality</term>, and **Mars** <note>original: "Kuja"</note> is at the end. If Mars is also positioned in the **"hole" cell** <term>Chidra-koshta; a specific dangerous house or square in the astrological grid</term> at the bottom, and if—within the **Three Banners** <term>Pataki-traya; a diagnostic chart</term>—the **Lord of the Year** <term>Varshesha; the planet ruling the individual's current year</term> is a malefic, then the individual "attains five-foldness" <note>original: "panchatvam"; a poetic Sanskrit idiom for death, referring to the body returning to the five elements</note>. 

So says the **Statement of Ratna** <note>referring to an authority or a text titled Ratnamala</note>: 
When Jupiter is pierced by malefics, and there is a zero <gloss>not</gloss> there <unclear>Mars</unclear>, then at the end, the Time of Mars <note>Death</note> arrives, even as one laughs and plays...

---

->When Jupiter is found with two zeros, and Mars is at the end,<-
->The loss of life will occur in that year, if joined by a malefic. 76<-
->When Mars is found with two zeros, or if<-
->Mars occurs there specifically, death is to be known in a<-
->year joined by a malefic. 77<-

---

Explanation of verse 76: "Two-fold" means when, at the beginning of the **Five Vowels**, **Mars** <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> is characterized by two zeros and Mars also occurs at the end. If Mars, arising from the **Three Banners**, is in the "hole" cell at the bottom position, and the year is joined by a malefic, then death must certainly be known. As it is said: "When Mars is in the zero and Mars has gone to the final position..." 77.

### Another Combination

This is also according to the **Sayings of Khana** <term>Khana-vakya; ancient astrological aphorisms attributed to the legendary female astronomer Khana</term>: "Where two malefics, a six, and a zero appear... with Mars at the end... the breath <note>original: "hamsa"; literally 'swan', a metaphor for the soul or breath</note> will certainly depart." 84

<summary>This page details specific planetary and numerical combinations (yogas) that predict death, focusing on the roles of the Moon, Rahu, Saturn, and Mars within the Pataki and Pancha-svara systems.</summary>

<keywords>Pataki Svarodaya, Moon (Vidhu), Rahu, Mars (Kuja), death (mrityu), malefic planets (papa), Khana-vakya, numerical grids (koshta)</keywords>

--- Page 110 ---
<meta>This page concludes the sequence of sixteen specific astrological configurations (yogas) that predict death. It focuses on the numerical count of planets—specifically Mercury and Mars—within the diagnostic charts of the Pataki Svarodaya system, ending this particular technical section of the manuscript.</meta>

### Another Combination

The author describes **another combination** <note>original: "yogāntaram āha"</note>. Regarding the **Five Sounds** <term>Panchasvara; an astrological system based on five primary vowels</term>:

In the configuration of the **third form**, if there are **three Mercuries** <term>Budha-traya</term> and, in the form of the third digit, **two Mars** <term>Bhauma-dvaya</term>, and the time periods <term>dasha</term> are void or diminished; and if in the **Three Banners** <term>Pataki-traya</term> the sequence is **Saturn, the Sun, and Mars** <note>original: "Shani-Ravi-Bhauma"</note>, then the state of **Rangarvi** <note>a technical term for a specific planetary clash</note> occurs. In that case, **death** <term>mrityu</term> takes place.

And so the instructional sentence says: "By the calculation of **three Mercuries (33)**, **Mars**, and **Rahu**, death is spoken of in the third year." This is written without doubt. 
<gloss>Where the number 7 arrives, there it is collected.</gloss>

> ->If there are three Mercuries and two Mars, and the periods are void,<-
> ->and if in a year joined by Saturn, the Sun, and Mars, the 'Rangarvi' state occurs,<-
> ->then death is certain. 85<-
>
> ->When Mars is the fifth or the seventh in the Five Sounds,<-
> ->and the year is associated with three malefics,<-
> ->one should likewise predict death. 86<-

The calculation described is revealed as starting from the number 7. In this year, it is said <note>original: "vadati," meaning the text 'speaks' the prediction</note>. 85

Now the author describes **another combination** <note>original: "athānya yogam āha"</note>. Regarding the **Five Sounds**: if Mars <term>Bhauma</term> is the fifth or seventh vowel-digit, and there are **malefics** <term>papas; harmful planets</term> in the **Three Banners**, then death occurs. 

As it is said: "When Mars is situated in the middle of the action, and if it occurs in the third and first cells of the diagram, then the person will surely meet **death** <term>mrityu</term> in that year, provided two or three malefics are also present." 86

Thus the **sixteen combinations** <note>original: "shodasha yogah"</note> are completed. 16

<summary>This page details the final astrological "yogas" for predicting death, specifically involving counts of Mercury and Mars across the Panchasvara and Pataki charts. It marks the conclusion of a 16-part technical series on terminal planetary configurations.</summary>

<keywords>Mercury (Budha), Mars (Bhauma), Saturn (Shani), Sun (Ravi), death (mrityu), Panchasvara, Pataki, Rangarvi, sixteen combinations, Malefics</keywords>

--- Page 111 ---
<meta>This page concludes the astrological treatise known as the Panchasvara. It follows the discussion of specific planetary combinations (yogas) that indicate death, specifically focusing on the timing of these events through planetary periods (dashas) and the relationship between celestial bodies.</meta>

<margin>
Pancha. Sva. <note>Abbreviation for Panchasvara</note>
44
<note>pencil addition</note> 55
</margin>

Regarding the word **Malefic** <note>original: "pāpa"</note>: when the major period <term>dasha; a planetary cycle</term> of a malefic planet occurs, or if there is a sub-period <term>antardasha</term> of a malefic within it: if the lords of these major and sub-periods are in a state of **enmity** <note>original: "shatrutve"</note>, death occurs. If they are in a state of **friendship** <note>original: "mitratve"</note>, there is only distress. The meaning is that death occurs during the combination of an enemy's period even within a **benefic major period** <term>shubha-dasha</term>. This is also because the **birth-ascendant** <term>janma-lagna; the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</term> is "pierced" or afflicted by malefics, and the birth occurring in a "malefic half-watch" <note>original: "pāpa-yāmārddhe"</note> acts as a contributing factor to all death-predicting combinations. 87

Regarding the term **Astrologer** <note>original: "jyotirvid"</note>: it is easy to understand. In a year where there are many **cruel planets** <term>krura; another term for malefics like Mars or Saturn</term>—

> In the major period of a malefic planet,
> if there is a sub-period of a malefic,
> death occurs if they are in an "enemy combination,"
> but if in a "friend combination," there is no doubt <note>of survival/lesser distress</note>. 87
>
> Astrologers carefully observe the 
> remedial measures for the fierce planets. 
> From the "Five Sounds" <term>Panchasvara</term> system, 
> the end of the difficulty <note>original: "sangata," referring to a specific period of crisis</note> is determined. 88
>
> **Thus ends the text named Panchasvara,**
> **composed by Sri Prajapatidasa.**

—and the benefics are few and **diminished in brilliance** <note>original: "virashmayaḥ," literally "without rays"</note>, one should know there will be distress in all undertakings; of this there is no doubt. This is a clear statement. All the cruel planets, even if they are in their **exaltation** <term>uccha; the highest point of a planet's strength</term>, always bring death. Benefics in **debilitated signs** <term>nicha; the weakest point of a planet's strength</term> bring total ruin to men. If the **Lord of the Year** <term>varshapa</term> is a malefic in its own sign, but is joined or aspected by a benefic, it becomes auspicious.

->**Finished. Auspiciousness.**<-
->**Samvat 1953, the first day of the bright fortnight of Chaitra.**<-
<note>This date corresponds to March or April of 1896 CE.</note>
->**This book was copied by Pandit Kunjalala in the city of Jammu,**<-
->**belonging to Sri Raja Ram Singh Ji. 3**<-

<summary>This final folio of the Panchasvara concludes the prognostications for death based on planetary periods and provides a formal colophon. It identifies the author as Prajapatidasa and the scribe as Pandit Kunjalala, who completed the copy in 1896 CE in Jammu for Raja Ram Singh.</summary>
<keywords>Panchasvara, Prajapatidasa, Dasha, Antardasha, Malefic, Benefic, Jammu, Raja Ram Singh, 1896 CE, Astrological Conclusion</keywords>

**No.** 1828
**ka** <note>original: "क". A sub-classification letter, similar to "Volume A" or a specific section indicator.</note>

**Title:** **The Five Vowels** <note>original: "पञ्चस्वरा" (Pañcasvarā). This is a classical text on <term>phonetic astrology</term> (Svara Shastra), a system that uses the vowels in a person's name or the timing of an event to make predictions.</note>

**Author:** **Prajapatidasa** <note>original: "प्रजापतिदासः".</note>

**Extent:** **44 leaves** <note>original: "४४ पत्र" (patra). This indicates the manuscript consists of 44 individual handwritten folios.</note> **Age:** **Year 1953** <note>original: "सं. १९५३" (saṃvat 1953). This refers to the Vikrama Samvat calendar. To find the Western date, we subtract 57 years, making this approximately 1896–1897 CE.</note>

**Subject:** **<term>Astrology</term>** <note>original: "ज्योतिषम्" (Jyotiṣam). This term encompasses the traditional Indian science of time-keeping, astronomy, and divination.</note>

**Author** Prajapatidasa <note>original: "प्रजापतिदासः"</note>
**Extent** 44 leaves <note>original: "४४ पत्र" (*patra*). These are individual folios, likely made of handmade paper or birch bark.</note>  **Age** Era year 1853 <note>original: "सं. १८५३" (*Samvat 1853*). Using the Vikrama calendar, this date corresponds to approximately 1796–1797 CE.</note>
**Subject** <term>Astrology</term> <note>original: "ज्योतिषम्" (*Jyotisham*). In this context, it refers to the predictive sciences, including the study of omen-reading and phonetic divination.</note>

<note>The label identifies a Sanskrit manuscript titled "The Five Vowels" (*Panchasvara*) by the author Prajapatidasa. The work is classified under the broad category of *Jyotisham*, which encompasses astronomy and astrology. The manuscript consists of 44 individual leaves and was completed around 1796 CE. Faint, partially illegible markings on the red fabric backing—likely older archival stamps—include the numbers "6910" and "A. 05."</note>

<note>The Raghunatha Temple Library in Jammu remains one of the world's most vital repositories of Sanskrit literature. Established by Maharaja Ranbir Singh in the 19th century, it preserved thousands of texts from across the Indian subcontinent. This particular manuscript is a significant example of the "Svara Shastra" tradition, a branch of knowledge that bridges linguistics and divination, suggesting that the sounds of one's speech are deeply connected to their destiny.</note>

# Shri Raghunatha Temple Manuscript Library,
## JAMMU

---

**No.** 1828
**A** <note>original: "क" (*ka*). A Sanskrit letter used here as a serial marker, equivalent to the letter 'A' or 'Part 1'.</note>

**Title** <term>The Five Vowels</term> <note>original: "पञ्चस्वरा" (*Pañcasvarā*). An astrological and divinatory text that focuses on the *Svarodaya* system, which analyzes the sounds of vowels and the flow of breath to predict future outcomes.</note>

**Author** Prajapatidasa <note>original: "प्रजापतिदासः" (*Prajāpatidāsaḥ*).</note>

**Extent** 44 Leaves <note>original: "पत्र" (*patra*). This indicates the total number of physical folios or pages in the manuscript.</note> **Age** Era 1853 <note>original: "सं. १८५३" (*Saṃ. 1853*). This refers to the Vikrama Samvat calendar; the date corresponds to approximately 1796 CE.</note>

**Subject** <term>Astrology</term> <note>original: "ज्योतिषम्" (*Jyotiṣam*). A broad term for traditional Indian astral sciences, including astronomy, horoscopy, and various forms of divination.</note>

**Title** <term>Panchasvara</term> <note>original: "पञ्चस्वर" (*Pañcasvara*), literally "The Five Vowels." This is a specialized text on phonetic astrology (*Svarodaya*), which uses the sounds of speech and the patterns of breath to make prognostications.</note>

**Author** <term>Prajapatidasa</term> <note>original: "प्रजापतिदासः" (*Prajāpatidāsaḥ*).</note>

**Extent** 44 leaves <note>original: "४४ पत्र" (*44 patra*). These are the individual loose-leaf folios that comprise the physical manuscript.</note>

**Age** Samvat 1853 <note>original: "सं. १८५३" (*saṃ. 1853*). Based on the Vikrama Samvat calendar, this date corresponds to approximately 1796 CE.</note>

**Subject** <term>Astrology</term> <note>original: "ज्योतिषम्" (*jyotiṣam*). This term refers to the traditional Indian system of "the science of light," which includes astronomy, time-keeping, and divination.</note>

**Title** <term>Panchasvara</term> <note>original: "पञ्चस्वरा". Literally meaning "The Five Vowels," this is a treatise on *Svarodaya* astrology. It involves making predictions based on the phonetic sounds of a person's name and the rhythmic patterns of their breath.</note>

**Author** Prajapatidasa <note>original: "प्रजापतिदासः".</note>

**Extent** 44 leaves <note>original: "४४ पत्र" (44 patra). This specifies that the manuscript consists of 44 individual folios.</note> **Age** <term>Samvat 1853</term> <note>original: "सं. १८५३". This date from the Vikrama Samvat calendar corresponds to approximately 1796–1797 CE.</note>

**Subject** <term>Astrology</term> <note>original: "ज्योतिषम्" (jyotiṣam). This traditional Indian science encompasses astronomy, time-keeping, and divination.</note>

**No.** 1828 <gloss>A <note>original: "क" (ka). The first letter of the Sanskrit alphabet, used here to denote a sub-category or specific volume, equivalent to 'A'.</note></gloss>

**Title** **<term>The Five Vowels</term>** <note>original: "पञ्चस्वरा" (Pañcasvarā). A classic text in "Svara Shastra," a branch of divination that predicts the future based on the sounds of vowels and the flow of the practitioner's breath.</note>

**Author** **<term>Prajapatidasa</term>** <note>original: "प्रजापतिदासः".</note>

**Extent** 44 leaves <note>original: "४४ पत्र" (patra). This refers to the number of individual folios making up the manuscript.</note> **Age** <term>Year 1796–1797</term> <note>original: "सं. १८५३" (Samvat 1853). This date follows the Vikrama Samvat calendar, which began in 57 BCE.</note>

**Subject** **<term>Astrology</term>** <note>original: "ज्योतिषम्" (Jyotiṣam). In this context, it refers to the traditional Indian science of time-keeping, astronomy, and omen-reading.</note>

<unclear>Dhi 28</unclear> <note>original: "धी २८". A classification code, likely indicating the cabinet or shelf location within the library.</note>

**No.** 2828
**A** <note>original: "क" (ka). The first letter of the Sanskrit alphabet, used here as a serial marker or volume category, similar to "Part 1."</note>

**Title** **<term>The Five Vowels</term>** <note>original: "पञ्चस्वरा" (Panchasvara). This is a specialized astrological system that uses the five primary Sanskrit vowels (a, i, u, e, o) to predict the outcome of events, health, or conflicts based on the phonetics of names or the time of inquiry.</note>

**Author** **<term>Prajapatidasa</term>** <note>A traditional Indian scholar known for his works on divination and technical sciences.</note>

**Extent** 44 leaves <note>original: "पत्र" (patra). This indicates the manuscript consists of 44 individual folios or hand-written pages.</note> **Age** <term>1796–1797 CE</term> <note>original: "सं. १८५३" (Samvat 1853). This date is recorded in the Vikrama Samvat era, the traditional calendar system of northern India.</note>

**Subject** **<term>Astrology</term>** <note>original: "ज्योतिषम्" (Jyotisham). A broad discipline in Sanskrit literature encompassing astronomy, mathematics, and various forms of divination.</note>

Dated to 1796–1797 CE, the manuscript is a preserved specimen of late 18th-century scientific writing from the Shri Raghunatha Temple collection in Jammu.</summary>
